```
def obj(z): return smooth_obj_val(z,beta)
def grad(z): return smooth_grad(z,beta)
res = minimize(lambda z: obj(z), z0, jac=grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=zmin,zmax, options={'maxiter':..., 'maxfun':..., 'gtol':...})
```

Custom update:
```
for T in schedule:
  for iter:
    c, M = conv(p)
    if score improve: store
    idx = argsort(c)[-T:]
    W = zeros; W[idx]=1/T
    # G = 2 ∑ W_k p_reflected
    # direct if T small else FFT
    grad_p = compute_grad(p, W)
    grad_z = p * (grad_p - dot(p, grad_p))  # softmax gradient
    # scale by n^2 maybe
    g = grad_z * scale
    # Adam: m,v update
    z -= lr * m/(sqrt(v)+eps)
    z -= z.mean()
```

Let's design final code with multi-phase:
A. Generate candidate library:
   - For several n values (e.g., 512, 1024, 2048, 4096 maybe 6144) and α parameters (0.5,1.0,1.5) create geometric.
   - Also create "block/power" shapes: p_i ∝ (i+1)^a (n-1-i)^b with a,b from set [-0.5,-0.2,0,0.2,0.5] but ensure no central blow? If a,b negative, endpoints high; central maybe? We'll try.
   - Perhaps mix: p_i = q * (i+1)^a + (1-q)*(n-i+1)^b.
   But simpler: geometric family enough?
   - Evaluate scores via FFT, keep best few.
B. For each top initial, run mirror descent optimization with T/tau annealing.
   - Use n adapted: maybe 2048 for speed; but can try 4096 if time remains.
   - Time budget allocation: total 2400s; reserve 60s final exact eval. We must ensure return before deadline. Use time.monotonic.

Let's design final code with:

Let's outline code:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import math
import sys
from scipy.optimize import minimize? maybe not necessary but can import.

# maybe configure numpy errstate.

def _fft_len(n):
    m=1
    while m < 2*n-1: m*=2
    return m

def normalize(p):
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.ones_like(p)/p.size
    p = p/ s
    return p

def conv_coef(a, L=None):
    if L is None: L=_fft_len(a.size)
    f=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f, L)[:2*a.size-1]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c

def internal_score(a):
    p=a.astype(np.float64)
    s=p.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.inf
    p=p/s
    c=conv_coef(p)
    m=float(c.max())
    return 2.0*p.size*m
```
For exact final, call evaluate_sequence([float...]) maybe O(n^2), but internal should match.

Initial shapes:
```
def make_geometric(n, alpha):
    r=math.exp(-alpha/n)
    i=np.arange(n)
    p=np.exp(-alpha*i/n)
    # or use r**i can underflow for large alpha*n? alpha small up to3 => exp(-3) fine.
    p/=p.sum()
    return p

def make_power_asym(n, a, b):
    i=np.arange(1,n)
    left=((i+0.5)/n)**a maybe avoid endpoint zero; right=(1 - i/n)**b
    p=left*right
    normalize
```
But power at endpoints if exponent negative can have p0 infinite if i=0. Use (i+1) not issue. We'll generate a,b.

```
def make_block_exp(n, blocks params):piecewise constant exponential blocks; but maybe not.

Optimization:
```
def run_gradient_search(p0, budget, seed, mode):
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    n=p0.size
    p=p0.copy(); p=normalize(p)
    z=np.log(p + 1e-300)
    z-=z.mean()
    L=_fft_len(n)
    pF? We recompute each iter.
    m_mom=zeros; v_mom=zeros
    lr=0.1? adapt.
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=internal_score(p)
    tau_frac=0.25; T_init=maybe n//32
    schedule:
       epochs with tau from 0.5 down 0.02, T = max(1, int(n*frac))
    for each tau:
      for iter until time:
         c=conv_coef(p, L)
         M=c.max()
         score=2*n*M
         if score<best_score: best...
         # weights
         if T<=0 or use exp: 
             diff=M-c
             # tau_rel = tau_frac*M
             s=-diff/tau_rel
             # clip s min -50
             w=np.exp(np.clip(s, -50,0))
             wsum=w.sum()
             if wsum<=0: top...
             w/=wsum
         else:
             top = np.argpartition(c, -T)[-T:]
             w=zeros; w[top]=1/T; then maybe weight by c? Could uniform or exp within top.
         # compute grad_p
         if w_nonzero_count < threshold: direct
         else: direct? For n=2048, T=64 direct fine; if T large 256 maybe still okay (524k operations) per iter, maybe okay. But Python loop over 256 with slices can be costly; use FFT if w count >200.
         # direct:
         grad=np.zeros(n)
         nz=np.nonzero(w)[0]
         # if not too many, loop:
         for k in nz:
            wk=w[k]
            start=max(0, k-(n-1)); end=min(n-1,k)+1
            if start<end:
                other=k - np.arange(start,end)
                grad[start:end] += wk * p[other]
         grad *=2
         # FFT: need p_rev and w; length Lg=1<<(3*n-1).bit_length? Actually convolution length n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2. For n=2048, 8192. Use rfft.
         # But if w sparse, direct.
         A=np.dot(p, grad)
         grad_z = p*(grad - A)
         # scale gradient
         scale = float(n*n)  # maybe /2
         g = grad_z * scale
         # Adam update
         m_mom = beta1*m + (1-beta1)g
         v_mom = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
         m_hat=m/(1-beta1**t)
         v_hat=v/(1-beta2**t)
         z -= lr * m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
         z -= z.mean()
         # z clip
         z=np.clip(z, -40, 40)
         # compute p
         z-=z.max()
         p=np.exp(z)
         p/=p.sum()
         # if nonfinite break
    # local search
    p=local_search(p, budget_left)
    return p,best_score
```

Gradient direct via FFT:
```
def weighted_gradient(p, w, L):
    p_rev_fft = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], L)
    w_fft = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(p_rev_fft * w_fft, L)
    return 2.0 * conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```

```
def build_conv_matrix(p):
    n=len(p); K=2*n-1
    F=np.empty((K,n))
    for k in range(K):
       start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)+1
       cols=np.arange(start,end)
       idx=k-cols
       F[k,start:end]=p[idx]
    return F
```

Local refine implementation:
```
def local_refine(p0, budget_s):
    p=np.asarray(p0,float); p=normalize(p)
    n=p.size; L=...
    best_score=internal_score(p)
    curr=p.copy(); curr_score=best_score
    rng=default_rng(123)
    sigma=0.1
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    temp=0.05
    i=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       mode=rng.random()
       cand=curr.copy()
       if mode<0.4: # single index
           idx=int(rng.integers(n))
           factor=math.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
           cand[idx]*=factor
       elif mode<0.7: # block
           width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(64, n//16))))
           st=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1)))
           factor=math.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
           cand[st:st+width]*=factor
       else: # pair anti-symmetric? Choose idx1 idx2, adjust to make more imbalanced relative to top?
           i1=int(rng.integers(n)); i2=int(rng.integers(n))
           transfer = (cand[i2]-cand[i1])*rng.uniform(0,0.1)
           cand[i1]+=transfer; cand[i2]-=transfer
       cand=np.maximum(cand,0); S=cand.sum(); if S<=0: continue
       cand/=S
       score=internal_score(cand)
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=score; best=cand.copy()
       # accept: if score < curr_score or random temp
       if score < curr_score or rng.random() < math.exp(-(score-curr_score)/(temp*max(curr_score,1e-12))):
           curr=cand; curr_score=score
       else:
           # sometimes reset to best to avoid drift
           if rng.random()<0.05:
               curr=best.copy(); curr_score=best_score
       # anneal temp and sigma
       sigma = max(0.01, sigma*0.999)
       temp = max(0.01*curr_score, temp*0.999)
       i+=1
    return best, best_score
```
Simulated annealing accepting worse might wander, but keep best. `internal_score` is FFT O(log) so fast. But many calls per second maybe ~500; in 500s 250k moves, okay? Python overhead plus FFT length 4096 maybe 0.002s => 200/s; 100k in 500s. Good.

Need ensure scores finite. If current score high, acceptance for worse maybe too high; temperature scaled by score ~1.5. temp initial 0.01? Use set `temp=0.02` (since score differences ~0.01). In internal score units. We'll tune.

Implement directed local move using current c:
- Store current c, M, idxmax set top T (e.g., 10).
- For random active k in top, choose a random pair (i, j=k-i) where both valid and maybe both positive. Currently c_k = sum p_i p_j. To reduce max while preserving sum, transfer mass from the larger of the pair to the smaller? Wait product with fixed sum s is minimized by making one as small as possible. If we transfer from the larger to the smaller, product decreases if larger> smaller? For p_i+p_j constant s, product p_i p_j. Let p_i > p_j. If we decrease p_i by δ and increase p_j by δ, product changes: derivative 2(p_j-p_i)<0, so product decreases until equal? Actually starting p_i large, moving towards equal decreases product? p=2,0 product0; moving to 1.5,0.5 product0.75 increases. So product is quadratic with minimum at extremes (0,s), maximum at equal. To reduce product, move away from equal towards extremes. Which direction? If p_i>p_j, increasing p_i and decreasing p_j moves away from equal (towards extreme) and reduces product. If p_i<p_j, decrease p_i? More generally, adjust to increase difference: add δ to max, subtract δ from min. This reduces their product. But other coefficients affected. We can do this for active pairs.
- However sum constraint: moving mass from min to max (increase max, decrease min) keeps pair sum but changes other pair sums; may increase smaller lags. We can accept if score improves.
- So directed move: choose active k, choose i in valid range; j=k-i. If i==j skip. If p[i]>p[j], cand[i]+=δ, cand[j]-=δ; else cand[i]-=δ, cand[j]+=δ (make difference larger). δ = min(p[li], p[hi]) * fraction? To not zero, δ = alpha * min(p_i,p_j), alpha ~0.1-0.5. Or transfer from smaller to larger. This anti-equalizes. Similarly could move mass between arbitrary indices.
- This move may improve. Include as mode.

But current c needed. We can compute initial c in local and update after accepted move. For rejected, no c update. To avoid stale, when current changes, compute c at accept. For simulation acceptance, we compute candidate score and c; if accept, set c. Good.

Local refine function can be:

def local_refine(p0, budget_s, seed=77):
   p=normalize; c=conv; score...
   best...
   temp=0.005? sigma=0.1
   top_T=8
   while time:
      # choose mode
      if mode<0.35: active pair move
          if c is None: compute
          top = np.argpartition(c, -T)[-T:]
          k=top[rand]
          valid_start=max(0,k-n+1); valid_end=min(n-1,k)+1
          i=int(rng.integers(valid_start, valid_end))

          cand=copy; cand[hi]+=delta; cand[lo]-=delta; normalize? Sum unchanged but due floating maybe.
      else random scale etc.
      score=internal_score(cand)
      ...

Let's detail helper functions.

`_next_pow2(m)`:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    r=1
    while r < m: r <<=1
    return r
```
Could use int bit_length: `return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()` if m>1.

`softmax`:
```
def _softmax(z):
    z=np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    zmax=z.max()
    e=np.exp(z-zmax)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones_like(z)/z.size
    return e/s
```
Clip z before.

`convolve_coeffs(p, L=None)`:
```
def convolution_coeffs(p, L=None):
    n=p.size
    if L is None: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    f=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f, L)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    c= c / c.sum() ? Wait if p sum1, sum c=1. Due FFT errors, sum maybe 0.999999; not needed. But for weights, maybe small negative zero. Use maximum.
    return c
```
For very small n (n=1?), handle: conv length1 [1]. score=2*1*1=2. But n likely large. Add n=1.

`internal_score_from_c(p,c)`: `2*p.size*float(np.max(c))`.

`weighted_gradient_fft(p,w,L,n)`:
```
def weighted_gradient_fft(p,w,L):
    n=p.size
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    # ensure w length 2n-1
    wr = np.zeros(L)
    wr[:len(w)] = w
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, L)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(wr, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fw, L)
    return 2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Could reuse p_rev FFT? We compute each iter. Maybe optimize by computing `Fp_rev` once? p changes each iter, so no.

Could compute p_rev via negative indices: `p[::-1]` makes copy? It returns view with negative stride; rfft will copy. Fine.

`make_weights(c, tau_frac)`:
```
def make_weights(c, tau_frac):
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if M <=0: return np.ones(len(c))/len(c)
    tau = max(M*tau_frac, 1e-300)
    s = (c - M)/tau
    s = np.clip(s, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(s)
    ws = w.sum()
    if ws <=0 or not finite:
       top = np.argmax(c); w=zeros; w[top]=1
    else: w/=ws
    return w
```
If tau large, weights broad; if tau small, top.

`normalize_nonneg(p)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(p):
   p=np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
   p=np.clip(p,0.0,1e30?) # no need
   s=p.sum()
   if not finite or s<=0: p=np.full_like(p,1.0); s=p.size
   p/=s
   return p
```
Clamping to 0 can create zeros; if all zero, uniform.

`internal_score(p)`:
```
def internal_score(p):
   p=normalize_nonneg(p)
   c=convolution_coeffs(p)
   M=float(np.max(c))
   return 2.0*p.size*M
```

def initial_geometric(n, alpha=1.0):
    x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
    p=np.exp(-alpha*x)
    p=normalize_nonneg(p)
    return p
```

def initial_smooth_random(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    alpha = rng.uniform(0.2, 2.0)
    x = np.arange(n)/float(n)
    z = -alpha*x + rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)*x + rng.normal(0,0.2,n)
    # smooth noise: cumulative sum with small? Could do z += 0.1*np.convolve(z, np.ones(7)/7, mode='same')
    z -= z.mean()
    p=np.exp(np.clip(z,-5,5)); p/sum
    return p
```

def score_with_conv(p):

`resample_sequence(p, new_n)`: use cumulative distribution? Since p is a probability on grid, to change length while preserving distribution shape, we can interpolate the continuous density underlying p. Approach: treat p_i as mass at positions i/(n-1). Create new grid positions j/new_n, linearly interpolate p (or density) and multiply by? If p_i are weights (probability mass), to resample to a new length, we want density f = p_i / Δx. For linear interpolation of mass: new_p[j] = value_at_pos * Δx_new? Simpler: use `np.interp(new_positions, old_positions, p, left=0, right=0)` then normalize. Since p are probability masses, if Δx changes, interpolation of values p not density; but normalize afterward, and for smooth shapes shape preserved. Fine. Or use density: old_density=p*old_n, interpolate density, new_p=density_interp/new_n. Normalize. Both same after normalization if endpoints? Use density.
```
def resample_length(p, new_n):
   n=p.size
   if n==new_n: return p.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
   density = p * n
   dens_new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, density, left=density[0], right=density[-1])
   p_new=dens_new/new_n
   return normalize_nonneg(p_new)
```

Let's write optimize_chunk carefully with time checks.

```
def optimize_chunk(state, max_seconds):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max_seconds
    n=state['n']; L=state['L']; p=state['p']; z=state['z']; m=state['m']; v=state['v']
    tau=state['tau']; lr=state['lr']; beta1=state['b1']; beta2=state['b2']; iter=state['iter']; no_imp=state['no_improve']
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c=convolution_coeffs(p, L)
        M=float(np.max(c))
        score=2.0*n*M
        state['current_score']=score
        if score < state['best_score'] - 1e-13:
            state['best_score']=score
            state['best_p']=p.copy()
            no_imp=0
            state['iter']=iter
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            # slow cool
            if no_imp > 50:
                tau = max(state['min_tau'], tau * (1 - min(0.05, no_imp*1e-6)))
                # maybe lr slight down?
            # perturb if stuck
            if no_imp > 4000:
                # reset from best with noise
                p = state['best_p'].copy()
                z = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); z-=z.mean()
                rng = np.random.default_rng(state['seed'] + iter)
                z += rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n)
                z = np.clip(z, -30, 30); z-=z.mean()
                p = _softmax(z)
                m = np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
                tau=0.35; lr=0.02; no_imp=0; iter=0
        # weights
        w = _make_weights(c, tau)
        # gradient
        nz = np.flatnonzero(w > 1e-300)
        if nz.size == 0: break
        if nz.size < 128:
            grad_p = _weighted_gradient_direct(p, w, nz)
        else:
            grad_p = _weighted_gradient_fft(p, w, L)
        A = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
        grad_z = p * (grad_p - A)
        scale = float(n) * float(n)
        g = grad_z * scale
        nrms = math.sqrt(float(np.mean(g*g)))
        if not math.isfinite(nrms) or nrms <= 1e-18:
            break
        g = g / nrms
        m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
        v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
        step = lr * m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-12)
        # limit step magnitude maybe
        step_norm = math.sqrt(float(np.mean(step*step)))
        max_step=0.05
        if step_norm > max_step:
            step = step * (max_step/step_norm)
        z = z - step
        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
        z -= z.mean()
        p = _softmax(z)
        iter +=1
    state['p']=p; state['z']=z; state['m']=m; state['v']=v; state['tau']=tau; state['lr']=lr; state['iter']=iter; state['no_improve']=no_imp
```
We update state current score at start; after final p maybe not evaluated; next chunk will. At end if time, okay. The loop condition uses `time.monotonic()` each iteration, fine.

 'best_p':p.copy(),'best_score':score,'current_score':score,'no_improve':0,'seed':seed
}

Local refine: Need use internal score. We'll implement `local_refine(p0, max_seconds, seed=123)` returning p,score. It can operate on normalized p. Use current c to propose active moves.

```
def local_refine(p0, max_seconds, seed=123):
   p=normalize_nonneg(p0)
   n=p.size; L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
   score,c=score_with_conv(p)
   best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score; curr=p.copy(); curr_score=score; curr_c=c
   if not finite: return uniform, inf
   rng=default_rng(seed)
   sigma=0.12; temp=0.005; top_T=min(16, 2*n-1)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+max_seconds
   while time < deadline:
      cand=curr.copy()
      u=rng.random()
      if u<0.35:
         # active pair move
         if curr_c is None or not finite: compute
         # choose top lag
         if top_T >= len(curr_c): top=np.arange(len(curr_c)) else argpartition
         k=int(top[rng.integers(top_T)])
         start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)+1
         if start>=end: continue
         i=start + int(rng.integers(end-start))
         j=k-i
         if i==j: continue
         if curr[i] <= 0 or curr[j] <=0:
             # maybe scale one
             factor=math.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma)); cand[i]*=factor; ...
             pass
         if curr[i] > curr[j]: hi=i; lo=j else hi=j;lo=i
         minv=min(curr[hi], curr[lo])
         if minv > 0:
            frac=rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45)
            delta=minv*frac
            cand[hi]+=delta; cand[lo]-=delta
      elif u<0.70:
          # scale random index/block
          if rng.random()<0.5:
             idx=int(rng.integers(n)); cand[idx]*=math.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
          else:
             maxw=max(2, min(128, n//16)); width=int(rng.integers(1,maxw)); st=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1))); cand[st:st+width]*=math.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
      else:
          # random interpolation between best and geometric? Maybe perturbation
          idx1=int(rng.integers(n)); idx2=int(rng.integers(n))
          delta=(curr[idx2]-curr[idx1])*rng.uniform(-0.2,0.2)
          cand[idx1]+=delta; cand[idx2]-=delta
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum()
      if s<=0 or not finite: continue
      cand/=s
      sc, cc=score_with_conv(cand)
      if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
          best_score=sc; best_p=cand.copy()
      # accept
      if sc < curr_score - 1e-15 or (temp > 1e-9 and rng.random() < math.exp(-(sc-curr_score)/max(temp,1e-12))):
          curr=cand; curr_score=sc; curr_c=cc
      elif rng.random() < 0.03:
          # reset to best sometimes
          curr=best_p.copy(); curr_score=best_score; curr_c=None
      sigma=max(0.01, sigma*0.9995)
      temp=max(1e-3, temp*0.9995)
      # If no best improvement many? keep.
   return best_p,best_score
```
Need ensure `cand[lo]-=delta` not negative; delta≤minv*0.45 so positive. For random transfer, may negative; clip after.

`score_with_conv` returns c; for current c after reset None, recompute. In active move, if curr_c None, compute `_, curr_c = score_with_conv(curr)`.

```
def initial_geometric(n, alpha):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(max(1,n))
    p = np.exp(-alpha*x)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)
```

def initial_curve(n, a,b,c):
    x=...
    z=a*x + b*x*x + c*x*x*x
    # clip to avoid negative exp huge
    z=np.clip(z, -5.0, 5.0)
    p=np.exp(z)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)
```

```
def make_random_starts(seed):
    starts=[]
    ns = [1024, 2048, 4096]
    for n in ns:
       for alpha in (0.4,0.8,1.2,1.6): starts.append(initial_geometric(n,alpha))
    for n in (2048,4096):
       for s in range(1,4):
           rng=default_rng(seed+1000*n+s)
           a=rng.uniform(-3.0,-0.1)
           b=rng.uniform(-1.5,1.5)
           c=rng.uniform(-1.5,1.5)
           p=initial_curve(n,a,b,c)
           # random block mod
           blocks=int(rng.integers(3,8)); centers=some; multiply
           starts.append(p)
    return starts
```

Pseudo final code structure:
```
"""Docstring"""
import numpy as np
import math
import time
from operator import itemgetter

# helpers...

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)
    deadline = start_time + total_budget
    # generate starts
    starts = generate_starts(seed)
    # info list
    info=[]
    for p in starts:
       p=normalize_nonneg(p)
       sc=internal_score(p)
       if finite: info.append((sc, p.size, p))
    if not info: fallback uniform
    info.sort(key=itemgetter(0,1))
    quotas={1024:2, 2048:6, 4096:4}
    selected=[]; count={}
    for sc,n,p in info:
       q = quotas.get(n, 2)
       c = count.get(n,0)
       if c >= q: continue
       selected.append(p); count[n]=c+1
       if len(selected) >= 12: break
    # ensure if not global maybe add height_sequence? already in info.
    states=[]
    for idx,p in enumerate(selected):
       state = make_state(p, seed+idx)
       states.append(state)
    global_best_p=states[0]['best_p']; global_score=states[0]['best_score']
    for st in states[1:]: if st['best_score']<... update
    # print
    # rounds
    rounds = [(8, 40, 0.35), (6, 70, 0.25), (4, 130, 0.15)]? maybe dynamic.
    # But states length maybe 12. We'll sort and run.
    for round_idx,(n_states,per) in enumerate([(8,40),(6,70),(4,140)]):
       if time >= deadline: break
       # maybe re-add state from global best if new
       # sort
       states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))
       top = states[:n_states]
       # if number top small, allocate remaining evenly? Use per limited by remaining.
       for st in top:
          if time>=deadline: break
          sub = min(per, max(5.0, (deadline-time.monotonic())/max(1, len(top))))
          optimize_chunk(st, sub)
          update global
          print
       # if improved, add noise state from best maybe if len<12 and improved_round
       if global_score < 1.55 and len(states)<12:
          p=global_best_p.copy()
          p=normalize_nonneg(p)
          rng=...
          pert=...
          states.append(make_state(pert, seed+int(round(1e6*global_score)) ))
    # if global best n can be improved by resampling
    if time remains > 150:
       target_n = 4096 if global_best_p.size < 4096 else global_best_p.size
       if target_n != global_best_p.size:
          p_res = resample_length(global_best_p, target_n)
          state=make_state(p_res, seed+999)
          optimize_chunk(state, min(300, deadline-now))
          update global
          p_res2 = resample_length(global_best_p, 3809) if target_n !=3809
       # local refine
    remaining=max(0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 20)
    if remaining > 30:
       p_best, sc_best = local_refine(global_best_p, min(remaining, 1200), seed=12345)
       if sc_best < global_score: update
       print
    # if still time maybe local refine second best state
    remaining=...
    if remaining > 120 and len(states)>1:
       # second best state p
       states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))
       p2=states[1]['best_p']
       p2_ref, sc2 = local_refine(p2, remaining, seed=54321)
       update
    # final candidate
    best_p=normalize_nonneg(global_best_p)
    seq=[float(x) for x in best_p]
    try:
       exact=evaluate_sequence(seq)
       if not finite: exact=global_score

```
def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```

`convolution_coeffs` if p.size==1: p sum1 -> c=[1]. Return `np.array([1.0])`. Ensure L? If L None.

```
def convolution_coeffs(p, L=None):
    n=p.size
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    f=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f, L)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    # optionally renormalize c? If sumc >0: c/=c.sum() maybe can alter M if p sum1 and FFT error. But if normalize c, M changes small. Since p sum1, sum c should be1; dividing enforces. However if p negative? no. We can do:
    s=c.sum()
    if s>0 and finite: c *= (1.0/s)
    return c
```
Wait if p is sum1 but not normalized? convolution_coeffs expects normalized? We'll ensure normalized. Renormalize c might hide errors but won't hurt. If p sum1 exactly, sum c ~1. Dividing by sum could slightly reduce M if sum>1; positive. But internal score invariant if p sum not 1? We always normalize p. Good.

`score_and_conv(p)`:
```
def score_and_conv(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).copy()
   p=np.clip(p,0.0,None)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return np.inf, None
   p/=s
   L=_next_pow2(2*p.size-1)
   c=convolution_coeffs(p,L)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   return 2.0*p.size*m, c
```
Note `convolution_coeffs` renormalizes c to sum1; if p sum1 c sum1. Good.

`make_weights(c, tau_frac)`:
```
def make_weights(c, tau_frac):
    M = float(np.max(c))
    K = c.size
    if M <= 0 or not math.isfinite(M):
        w = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64); w /= K; return w
    tau = M * float(tau_frac)
    if tau <= 0 or not math.isfinite(tau): tau = M * 0.1
    s = (c - M) / tau
    s = np.clip(s, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(s)
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if ws <= 0 or not math.isfinite(ws):
        w = np.zeros(K); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else:
        w /= ws
    return w
```
If tau_frac huge, s ~0 and w uniform; okay.

`weighted_gradient_direct`:
```
def weighted_gradient_direct(p, w, nz):
    n=p.size
    grad=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in nz:
        wk=float(w[k])
        if wk <= 0: continue
        start = k - (n - 1)
        if start < 0: start = 0
        end = k + 1
        if end > n: end = n
        if start < end:
            first = k - start
            stop = k - end
            if stop < 0:
                other = p[first::-1]
            else:
                other = p[first:stop:-1]
            grad[start:end] += wk * other
    grad *= 2.0
    return grad
```
Check when stop==first? Not with length>0. If stop=first? For length1, stop=first-1. If stop negative and first=-? first>=0. Good. But if stop negative and first=0, slice p[0::-1] gives [0]. Good.
Potential issue: If stop=0 and first>0, `p[first:0:-1]` includes indices first down to1, length first, but expected length end-start? Example k=5,n=5, first=4, stop=0 -> p[4:0:-1] indices4,3,2,1 length4, expected j=1..4 length4, good. If stop=first? not.

`weighted_gradient_fft`:
```
def weighted_gradient_fft(p,w,L):
    n=p.size
    K=2*n-1
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    wr = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    wr[:K] = w[:K]
    f1 = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, L)
    f2 = np.fft.rfft(wr, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(f1 * f2, L)
    return 2.0 * conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```
No direct copy for p[::-1]? rfft reads. Fine.

`_softmax`:
```
def softmax_positive(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(z - zmax)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       return np.ones_like(z, dtype=np.float64) / z.size
    return e / s
```
If z contains nan, zmax nan; handle:
```
if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): z=np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=30, neginf=-30)
```
We'll clip before.

`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p=np.clip(p,0.0,1e100)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
       p=np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       if p.size ==0: p=np.ones(1)
       else: p = np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64)
       s=p.size
    p /= s
    return p
```
Clipping to 1e100 but then normalize; okay.

Resample:
```
def resample_length(p, new_n):
    old_n=p.size
    if old_n == new_n: return p.copy()
    if old_n ==1: return np.full(new_n,1.0/new_n)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old_n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
    density=p*old_n
    dens_new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, density, left=density[0], right=density[-1])
    new_p=dens_new/new_n
    return normalize_nonneg(new_p)
```
Interpolation preserves endpoint density. Good.

Initial curve:
```
def initial_curve(n,a,b,c):
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)/float(max(1,n))
    z = a*x + b*x*x + c*x*x*x
    z = np.clip(z, -6.0, 6.0)
    p=np.exp(z)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)
```
Maybe add random block random:
```
def _random_blocked_p(n, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    p=initial_geometric(n, float(rng.uniform(0.4,1.8)))
    nb=int(rng.integers(2,6))
    boundaries=np.sort(rng.uniform(0,1,nb+2))[1:-1]
    edges=np.concatenate(([0.0], boundaries, [1.0]))
    for i in range(len(edges)-1):
       s=int(edges[i]*n); e=int(edges[i+1]*n)
       if e>s:
          p[s:e] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0,0.15)))
    return normalize_nonneg(p)
```
Need no lambdas. Good.

Let's draft optimize_chunk thoroughly:
```
def optimize_chunk(state, max_seconds):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max_seconds
    n = state['n']; L = state['L']
    p = state['p']; z = state['z']; m = state['m']; v = state['v']
    tau = state['tau']; lr = state['lr']
    b1 = state['b1']; b2 = state['b2']
    min_tau = state['min_tau']
    i = state['iter']; no_imp=state['no_improve']; smooth_best=state['smooth_best']
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = convolution_coeffs(p, L)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        score = 2.0 * n * M
        state['current_score']=score
        if score < state['best_score'] - 1e-14:
            state['best_score']=score
            state['best_p']=p.copy()
            no_imp=0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp > 20:
                tau = min(tau * 0.999, min_tau if no_imp > 2000? no max)
```
Actually `max(min_tau, tau*0.999)`. If no_imp 1000 -> factor 0.37. Good.
Perturb:
```
            if no_imp > 2500:
                p = state['best_p'].copy()
                z = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); z-=z.mean()
                rng=np.random.default_rng(state['seed'] + i)
                z += rng.normal(0, 0.08, n)
                z = np.clip(z, -30, 30); z-=z.mean()
                p = softmax_positive(z)
                m = np.zeros(n); v = np.zeros(n)
                tau = 0.40; lr = 0.03; no_imp=0; i=0; smooth_best=state['smooth_best']
```
But arbitrary creating rng each perturb costly; okay rare. Could use state rng? Need state['rng']? We can store. Simpler use deterministic noise via `np.random.default_rng(int(state['seed']) ^ i)`. Good.
Continue if p more or less.

Weights:
```
        w = make_weights(c, tau)
        smooth_val = float(np.dot(w,c))
        if smooth_val < smooth_best - 1e-13:
            smooth_best = smooth_val
            no_imp = max(0, no_imp - 1)
        nz = np.flatnonzero(w > 1e-300)
        if nz.size ==0: break
        if nz.size < 128:
            grad_p = weighted_gradient_direct(p,w,nz)
        else:
            grad_p = weighted_gradient_fft(p,w,L)
        A = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
        grad_z = p * (grad_p - A)
        scale = float(n) * float(n)
        g = grad_z * scale
        mean_sq = float(np.mean(g*g))
        if not finite: break
        if mean_sq <=1e-30: break
        g = g / math.sqrt(mean_sq)
        m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
        v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(g*g)
        step = lr * m / (np.sqrt(v)+1e-12)
        step_norm = math.sqrt(float(np.mean(step*step)))
        max_step=0.05
        if step_norm > max_step: step *= max_step/step_norm
        z = z - step
        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
        z -= z.mean()
        p = softmax_positive(z)
        i +=1
    state['p']=p; state['z']=z; ...; state['iter']=i; state['no_improve']=no_imp; state['smooth_best']=smooth_best
```

def lp_refine(p0, max_seconds, target_len=1024):
   p = resample_length(p0, target_len) if p0.size != target_len else p0.copy()
   p = normalize
   score, c = score_and_conv(p)
   M = score / (2*target_len)  # since normalized sum1
   # If M maybe inf skip
   # build A
   A = build_conv_matrix(p, target_len)
   b = np.full(2*target_len-1, M)
   obj = -np.ones(target_len)
   bounds=[(0.0,None)] for _ in range(target_len)
   res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': int(max_seconds-30)})
   if res.success and res.x is not None:
      g = np.clip(res.x,0,None); T=g.sum(); if T>1:
         g/=T
         for t in [0.1,...]:
             cand = (1-t)*p + t*g; upgrade maybe to original length? If original length different, resample cand to original and maybe direct.
```

Let's implement `build_conv_matrix` efficiently. Loop k and slice:
```
def build_conv_matrix(p, n=None):
    n=p.size; K=2*n-1
    A=np.empty((K,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(K):
        start = max(0, k-n+1)
        end = min(n-1,k)+1
        if start < end:
            # indices j=start..end-1, other=k-j
            # Use slice assignment with reversed p
            stop = k - end
            if stop < 0:
               val = p[k-start::-1]
            else:
               val = p[k-start:stop:-1]
            A[k,start:end] = val
    return A
```
A rows for k have zeros elsewhere. For n=1024, A ~2M entries, 16MB. Good. Linprog may copy. If memory okay.

Could use sparse matrix to reduce memory? `scipy.sparse.csc_matrix`. HiGHS accepts sparse. Building sparse maybe more code but memory less. Scipy linprog with A_ub sparse supported. But dense may be faster for 1024. Use dense.

def lp_improve_candidate(p0, max_seconds, lp_len=1024):
    if linprog is None or max_seconds < 120:
        return p0, internal_score(p0)
    orig_n=p0.size
    p = resample_length(p0, lp_len) if orig_n != lp_len else p0.copy()
    p=normalize_nonneg(p)
    score, c=score_and_conv(p)
    M = score/(2*lp_len) if score finite else None
    if M is None: return p0, score
    # Try up to 2 iterations
    best_low=p; best_low_score=score
    for it in range(2):
        remaining = max_seconds - (time.monotonic()-start)
        if remaining < 60: break
        try:
            A=build_conv_matrix(p)
            b=np.full(2*lp_len-1, M)
            obj=-np.ones(lp_len)
            bounds=[(0.0,None)]*lp_len
            opts={'time_limit': int(max(30, remaining-40))}
            # some versions don't accept time_limit? It does; if not, exception.
            res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=opts)
        except Exception:
            break
        if not res.success or res.x is None:
            break
        g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        g=np.clip(g,0,None); T=float(g.sum())
        if not finite or T<=1+1e-8: break
        g/=T
        # blend candidates
        found=False
        for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0):
            cand=(1-t)*p + t*g
            cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
            sc, cc=score_and_conv(cand)
            if finite and sc < best_low_score-1e-12:
                best_low_score=sc; best_low=cand
                # Also maybe if t==1 update p? We'll continue with best cand for next LP
                p=cand? For next LP, use best cand. But careful if best_low t small, M current for next should use p update maybe best_low. Use p=best_low.copy(); M=best_low_score/(2*lp_len); found=True; break? Better evaluate all then choose best.
        # after loop, update p to best_low, M to best_low_score/(2*lp_len)
```

Helper functions before propose.

`_next_pow2` with int bit_length:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

def internal_score(p):
    sc, _ = score_and_conv(p)
    return sc
```

Now final code. We'll include docstring summarizing: combines density initialization, smoothed mirror descent on peak coefficients, LP step, active-pair local search, resampling.

```python
"""
Search for low-norm nonnegative sequences minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

Method:
1. Generate diverse asymmetric initial densities (geometric, curved, blocked,
   and optionally the provided global construction).
2. Run a smooth mirror-descent optimization on log-probabilities, using weights
   focused on the currently largest convolution coefficients.
3. Optionally apply a bounded LP step on a coarsened vector and resampling it
   back to the working length.
4. Polish the best candidates with an exact-FFT local search that explicitly
   anti-equalizes pairs of coordinates contributing to peak convolution lags.
5. Return the best finite sequence found.
"""

import numpy as np
import math
import time

try:
    from operator import itemgetter
except Exception:
    itemgetter = None

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_nonneg(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1e100)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        n = max(1, p.size)
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    p /= s
    return p


def softmax_positive(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=30.0, neginf=-30.0)
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(np.clip(z - zmax, -80.0, 80.0))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(z, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, z.size)
    return e / s


def convolution_coeffs(p, L=None):
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None:
        L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    f = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, L)
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    s = float(c.sum())
    if math.isfinite(s) and s > 0:
        c /= s
    return c


def score_and_conv(p):
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, None
    c = convolution_coeffs(p)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf, None
    return 2.0 * n * m, c


def internal_score(p):
    sc, _ = score_and_conv(p)
    return sc


def resample_length(p, new_n):
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    old_n = p.size
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if old_n == 0:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, new_n)
    if old_n == new_n:
        return p.copy()
    if old_n == 1:
        return np.full(new_n, 1.0 / max(1, new_n), dtype=np.float64)

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old_n, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n, dtype=np.float64)
    density = p * old_n
    dens_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, density, left=density[0], right=density[-1])
    new_p = dens_new / new_n
    return normalize_nonneg(new_p)


def make_weights(c, tau_frac):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    k = c.size
    if k == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(m) or m <= 0:
        w = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= k
        return w

    tau = m * float(tau_frac)
    if not math.isfinite(tau) or tau <= 0:
        tau = max(1e-300, m * 0.1)

    s = (c - m) / tau
    s = np.clip(s, -60.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(s)
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(k, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= ws
    return w


def weighted_gradient_direct(p, w, nz):
    n = p.size
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in nz:
        wk = float(w[k])
        if wk <= 0.0:
            continue
        start = k - (n - 1)
        if start < 0:
            start = 0
        end = k + 1
        if end > n:
            end = n
        if start < end:
            first = k - start
            stop = k - end
            if stop < 0:
                other = p[first::-1]
            else:
                other = p[first:stop:-1]
            grad[start:end] += wk * other
    grad *= 2.0
    return grad


def weighted_gradient_fft(p, w, L):
    n = p.size
    k = 2 * n - 1
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    wr = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    wr[:k] = w[:k]
    f1 = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, L)
    f2 = np.fft.rfft(wr, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(f1 * f2, L)
    return 2.0 * conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def initial_geometric(n, alpha):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    p = np.exp(-float(alpha) * x)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)


def initial_curve(n, a, b, c):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    z = float(a) * x + float(b) * x * x + float(c) * x * x * x
    z = np.clip(z, -6.0, 6.0)
    p = np.exp(z)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)


def random_blocked_p(n, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = int(n)
    p = initial_geometric(n, float(rng.uniform(0.35, 1.9)))
    nb = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
    bnds = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, nb + 2))[1:-1]
    edges = np.concatenate(([0.0], bnds, [1.0]))
    for i in range(len(edges) - 1):
        s = int(edges[i] * n)
        e = int(edges[i + 1] * n)
        if e > s:
            p[s:e] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.18)))
    return normalize_nonneg(p)


def get_global_start():
    g = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if g is None:
        return None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size < 3:
        return None
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return None
    p /= s
    if p.size > 10000:
        p = resample_length(p, 4096)
    return normalize_nonneg(p)


def generate_starts(seed):
    out = []

    gp = get_global_start()
    if gp is not None:
        out.append((gp, 2))

    n2 = 2048
    for a in (0.55, 0.85, 1.15, 1.45, 1.80):
        out.append((initial_geometric(n2, a), 0))

    n4 = 4096
    for a in (0.80, 1.10, 1.45):
        out.append((initial_geometric(n4, a), 0))

    if gp is not None and gp.size not in (n2, n4):
        gn = int(gp.size)
        if gn <= 10000:
            for a in (0.80, 1.10):
                out.append((initial_geometric(gn, a), 0))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 11)
    for nn in (n2, n4):
        p = initial_curve(
            nn,
            float(rng.uniform(-3.0, -0.2)),
            float(rng.uniform(-1.5, 1.5)),
            float(rng.uniform(-1.5, 1.5)),
        )
        out.append((p, 1))

    out.append((random_blocked_p(n2, seed + 5), 1))
    out.append((random_blocked_p(n4, seed + 9), 1))

    return out


def select_starts(start_items):
    if itemgetter is None:
        selected = [p for p, group in start_items]
        return selected[:10]

    info = []
    for p, group in start_items:
        p = normalize_nonneg(p)
        sc = internal_score(p)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            info.append((sc, group, p.size, p))
    info.sort(key=itemgetter(0, 1, 2))

    caps = {0: 7, 1: 3, 2: 2}
    counts = {0: 0, 1: 0, 2: 0}
    selected = []

    for sc, grp, n, p in info:
        c = counts.get(grp, 0)
        cap = caps.get(grp, 1)
        if c >= cap:
            continue
        selected.append(p)
        counts[grp] = c + 1
        if len(selected) >= 10:
            break

    if len(selected) < 10 and info:
        seen = set(id(x) for x in selected)
        for sc, grp, n, p in info:
            if id(p) in seen:
                continue
            selected.append(p)
            seen.add(id(p))
            if len(selected) >= 10:
                break

    if not selected:
        selected.append(initial_geometric(2048, 1.0))
    return selected


def make_state(p, seed):
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        p = np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64) / 2048.0
        n = 2048
    if n > 10000:
        p = resample_length(p, 4096)
        n = p.size

    score = internal_score(p)
    if not math.isfinite(score):
        p = np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64) / 2048.0
        n = 2048
        score = 2.0

    z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-30.0)
    z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))

    return {
        'p': p,
        'z': z,
        'm': np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64),
        'v': np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64),
        'n': n,
        'L': _next_pow2(2 * n - 1),
        'tau': 0.40,
        'min_tau': 0.02,
        'lr': 0.035,
        'b1': 0.85,
        'b2': 0.99,
        'iter': 0,
        'no_improve': 0,
        'smooth_best': np.inf,
        'best_p': p.copy(),
        'best_score': score,
        'current_score': score,
        'seed': int(seed),
    }


def optimize_chunk(state, max_seconds):
    max_seconds = float(max_seconds)
    if max_seconds <= 0.05:
        return

    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max_seconds

    n = state['n']
    L = state['L']

    p = state['p'].copy()
    z = state['z'].copy()
    m = state['m'].copy()
    v = state['v'].copy()

    tau = state['tau']
    min_tau = state['min_tau']
    lr = state['lr']
    b1 = state['b1']
    b2 = state['b2']
    it = state['iter']
    no_imp = state['no_improve']
    smooth_best = state['smooth_best']

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = convolution_coeffs(p, L)
        mval = float(np.max(c))
        score = 2.0 * n * mval
        state['current_score'] = score

        if math.isfinite(score) and score < state['best_score'] - 1e-14:
            state['best_score'] = score
            state['best_p'] = p.copy()
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp > 20:
                tau = max(min_tau, tau * 0.999)
            if no_imp > 120:
                lr = max(0.004, lr * 0.999)
            if no_imp > 2600:
                p = state['best_p'].copy()
                z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
                z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-30.0)
                z -= float(np.mean(z))
                rng = np.random.default_rng((state['seed'] * 1000003 + it) % (2**63 - 1))
                z += rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n)
                z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
                z -= float(np.mean(z))
                p = softmax_positive(z)
                m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                tau = 0.40
                lr = 0.035
                no_imp = 0
                it = 0

        w = make_weights(c, tau)
        smooth_val = float(np.dot(w, c))
        if math.isfinite(smooth_val) and smooth_val < smooth_best - 1e-14:
            smooth_best = smooth_val
            if no_imp > 60:
                no_imp = max(0, no_imp - 8)

        nz = np.flatnonzero(w > 1e-300)
        if nz.size == 0:
            break

        if nz.size < 96:
            grad_p = weighted_gradient_direct(p, w, nz)
        else:
            grad_p = weighted_gradient_fft(p, w, L)

        A = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
        grad_z = p * (grad_p - A)
        scale = float(n) * float(n)
        g = grad_z * scale

        mean_sq = float(np.mean(g * g))
        if not math.isfinite(mean_sq) or mean_sq <= 1e-30:
            break

        g = g / math.sqrt(mean_sq)

        m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * g
        v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (g * g)
        step = lr * m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-12)

        step_norm = math.sqrt(float(np.mean(step * step)))
        max_step = 0.06
        if math.isfinite(step_norm) and step_norm > max_step:
            step *= max_step / step_norm

        z = z - step
        z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=30.0, neginf=-30.0)
        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
        z -= float(np.mean(z))
        p = softmax_positive(z)

        it += 1

    state['p'] = p
    state['z'] = z
    state['m'] = m
    state['v'] = v
    state['tau'] = tau
    state['lr'] = lr
    state['iter'] = it
    state['no_improve'] = no_imp
    state['smooth_best'] = smooth_best
    return state


def build_conv_matrix(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    k = 2 * n - 1
    A = np.zeros((k, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for row in range(k):
        start = row - (n - 1)
        if start < 0:
            start = 0
        end = row + 1
        if end > n:
            end = n
        if start < end:
            first = row - start
            stop = row - end
            if stop < 0:
                val = p[first::-1]
            else:
                val = p[first:stop:-1]
            A[row, start:end] = val
    return A


def lp_improve_candidate(p0, max_seconds, seed=12345):
    if linprog is None:
        return p0, internal_score(p0)

    orig_p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    orig_n = orig_p.size

    max_seconds = float(max_seconds)
    if max_seconds < 90.0:
        return orig_p, internal_score(orig_p)

    lp_len = 512 if max_seconds < 260 else 1024
    p = resample_length(orig_p, lp_len)
    cur_score, _ = score_and_conv(p)
    if not math.isfinite(cur_score):
        return orig_p, internal_score(orig_p)

    best_orig = orig_p.copy()
    best_orig_score = internal_score(orig_p)
    best_low = p.copy()
    best_low_score = cur_score

    start_lp = time.monotonic()

    for iteration in range(2):
        remain = max_seconds - (time.monotonic() - start_lp)
        if remain < 60.0:
            break

        # If lp_len is too slow, reduce to 512 for the second attempt.
        length = lp_len
        if iteration == 1 and length > 512:
            length = 512
            p = resample_length(best_low, length)
            cur_score, _ = score_and_conv(p)
            if not math.isfinite(cur_score):
                break
            best_low = p.copy()
            best_low_score = cur_score

        mval = best_low_score / (2.0 * length)
        if not math.isfinite(mval) or mval <= 0:
            break

        try:
            A = build_conv_matrix(p)
        except Exception:
            break

        timeout = int(max(30, remain - 45))
        opts = {
            'time_limit': timeout,
            'maxiter': 30000,
        }
        try:
            res = linprog(
                -np.ones(length, dtype=np.float64),
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=np.full(2 * length - 1, mval, dtype=np.float64),
                bounds=[(0.0, None)] * length,
                method='highs',
                options=opts,
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        if (not res.success) or getattr(res, 'x', None) is None:
            break

        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.clip(g, 0.0, None)
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        total = float(g.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(total) or total <= 1.0 + 1e-8:
            break
        g /= total

        cand_best = p.copy()
        cand_best_score = cur_score

        for t in (0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00):
            cand = (1.0 - t) * p + t * g
            cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
            sc, _ = score_and_conv(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < cand_best_score - 1e-13:
                cand_best_score = sc
                cand_best = cand

        if cand_best_score < best_low_score - 1e-13:
            best_low = cand_best.copy()
            best_low_score = cand_best_score
            p = cand_best.copy()
            cur_score = cand_best_score
        else:
            break

    # Bring the improved low-resolution vector back to the original length
    # and take the best blend if it is better.
    if orig_n != best_low.size:
        cand_orig = resample_length(best_low, orig_n)
    else:
        cand_orig = best_low.copy()

    for alpha in (1.00, 0.80, 0.60, 0.40):
        cand = (1.0 - alpha) * orig_p + alpha * cand_orig
        cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
        sc = internal_score(cand)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_orig_score - 1e-13:
            best_orig_score = sc
            best_orig = cand

    return best_orig, best_orig_score


def local_refine(p0, max_seconds, seed=98765):
    max_seconds = float(max_seconds)
    p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        p = np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64) / 2048.0
        n = 2048

    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max_seconds
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_score, best_c = score_and_conv(p)
    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
        p = np.ones(2048, dtype=np.float64) / 2048.0
        n = 2048
        best_score, best_c = score_and_conv(p)

    best_p = p.copy()
    cur = p.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    cur_c = best_c.copy() if best_c is not None else None

    sigma = 0.14
    temp = 0.004
    top_T = min(16, 2 * n - 1)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = cur.copy()
        u = float(rng.random())

        if u < 0.48:
            # Active-pair anti-equalization: increase imbalance of a pair that
            # contributes to one of the current peak lags.
            if cur_c is None:
                _, cur_c = score_and_conv(cur)
            if cur_c is None:
                continue

            K = cur_c.size
            if top_T >= K:
                top = np.arange(K)
            else:
                top = np.argpartition(cur_c, -top_T)[-top_T:]
            k = int(top[int(rng.integers(top_T))])

            start = k - (n - 1)
            if start < 0:
                start = 0
            end = k + 1
            if end > n:
                end = n
            if start >= end:
                continue

            i = start + int(rng.integers(end - start))
            j = k - i
            if i == j:
                continue

            if cur[i] <= cur[j]:
                hi, lo = j, i
            else:
                hi, lo = i, j

            minv = float(min(cur[hi], cur[lo]))
            if minv > 0.0:
                delta = minv * float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.45))
                cand[hi] += delta
                cand[lo] -= delta

        elif u < 0.86:
            # Random multiplicative perturbation, single coordinate or block.
            if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
                idx = int(rng.integers(n))
                cand[idx] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
            else:
                maxw = max(2, min(160, n // 12))
                width = int(rng.integers(1, maxw))
                st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                cand[st:st + width] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))

        else:
            # Pair transfer.
            i = int(rng.integers(n))
            j = int(rng.integers(n))
            if i != j:
                delta = (cur[j] - cur[i]) * float(rng.uniform(-0.25, 0.25))
                cand[i] += delta
                cand[j] -= delta

        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
        cand = np.nan_to_num(cand, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        cand /= s

        sc, cc = score_and_conv(cand)
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            continue

        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = cand.copy()

        accept = False
        if sc < cur_score - 1e-14:
            accept = True
        elif temp > 1e-12:
            diff = sc - cur_score
            if diff < 0:
                accept = True
            else:
                tmp = diff / temp
                if tmp < 700.0:
                    accept = (float(rng.random()) < math.exp(-tmp))

        if accept:
            cur = cand
            cur_score = sc
            cur_c = cc
        elif float(rng.random()) < 0.015:
            cur = best_p.copy()
            cur_score = best_score
            cur_c = None

        sigma = max(0.006, sigma * 0.9995)
        temp = max(1e-5, temp * 0.9995)

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start_total = time.monotonic()
    work_seconds = max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    deadline = start_total + work_seconds

    print(f"[plan] work_seconds={work_seconds:.1f}", flush=True)

    starts = generate_starts(seed)
    selected = select_starts(starts)
    states = []
    for i, p in enumerate(selected):
        st = make_state(p, int(seed) + 1000 + 17 * i)
        states.append(st)

    if itemgetter is not None:
        states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))

    best_p = states[0]['best_p'].copy()
    best_score = states[0]['best_score']
    for st in states[1:]:
        if st['best_score'] < best_score:
            best_score = st['best_score']
            best_p = st['best_p'].copy()

    print(f"[init] best={best_score:.8f} len={best_p.size}", flush=True)

    rounds = [
        (8, 45.0),
        (6, 75.0),
        (5, 110.0),
        (3, 170.0),
    ]

    round_global_best = best_score
    for r_idx, (count, per_state) in enumerate(rounds):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        if states:
            if itemgetter is not None:
                states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))
            top = states[:count]
        else:
            top = []

        if not top:
            break

        for st in top:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= deadline:
                break
            sub = min(per_state, max(5.0, (deadline - now) / max(1, len(top))))
            optimize_chunk(st, sub)
            if st['best_score'] < best_score:
                best_score = st['best_score']
                best_p = st['best_p'].copy()
                print(f"[round {r_idx}] improved to {best_score:.8f} len={best_p.size}", flush=True)
                if best_score < round_global_best - 1e-8:
                    round_global_best = best_score
                    # Add one noisy continuation from the new incumbent, if room exists.
                    if len(states) < 12:
                        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) * 31 + r_idx)
                        z = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-300))
                        z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-30.0)
                        z += rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, len(best_p))
                        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 30.0)
                        z -= float(np.mean(z))
                        new_p = softmax_positive(z)
                        states.append(make_state(new_p, int(seed) + 50000 + r_idx))
                        if itemgetter is not None:
                            states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))

    # Optional bounded LP improvement on a coarsened grid.
    remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if remaining > 420.0:
        lp_budget = min(300.0, max(60.0, remaining - 300.0))
        print(f"[lp] trying bounded LP with budget={lp_budget:.1f}s", flush=True)
        try:
            lp_p, lp_score = lp_improve_candidate(best_p, lp_budget, seed=int(seed) + 777)
            if math.isfinite(lp_score) and lp_score < best_score:
                best_score = lp_score
                best_p = lp_p.copy()
                print(f"[lp] improved to {best_score:.8f} len={best_p.size}", flush=True)
            else:
                print("[lp] no improvement", flush=True)
        except Exception as ex:
            print(f"[lp] skipped due to {ex}", flush=True)

    # Upsample / resample to a faster power-of-two working length if there is
    # enough time; this can capture a smoother version of the best shape.
    remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if remaining > 330.0 and best_p.size != 4096:
        p4096 = resample_length(best_p, 4096)
        s4096 = internal_score(p4096)
        if math.isfinite(s4096) and s4096 < best_score + 0.15:
            st4 = make_state(p4096, int(seed) + 999)
            st4['best_score'] = min(st4['best_score'], s4096)
            if s4096 < st4['best_score']:
                st4['best_score'] = s4096
                st4['best_p'] = p4096
            opt_budget = min(200.0, max(0.0, remaining - 200.0))
            if opt_budget > 30.0:
                optimize_chunk(st4, opt_budget)
            if st4['best_score'] < best_score:
                best_score = st4['best_score']
                best_p = st4['best_p'].copy()
                print(f"[res4096] improved to {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
        else:
            print(f"[res4096] skipped or worse: {s4096:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Final deterministic-ish local refinement.
    remaining = max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 15.0)
    if remaining > 30.0:
        print(f"[local] final local refinement with {remaining:.1f}s", flush=True)
        p2, s2 = local_refine(best_p, remaining, seed=int(seed) + 123456)
        if math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best_score = s2
            best_p = p2.copy()
            print(f"[local] improved to {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # If another local pass from the next-best stored state is affordable, do it.
    remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0)
    if states and remaining > 150.0:
        if itemgetter is not None:
            states.sort(key=itemgetter('best_score'))
        for st in states[:2]:
            if st['best_score'] >= best_score:
                continue
            if remaining <= 90.0:
                break
            p3, s3 = local_refine(st['best_p'], min(120.0, remaining), seed=int(st['seed']) + 424242)
            if math.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_score:
                best_score = s3
                best_p = p3.copy()
                print(f"[local2] improved to {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
            remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0)

    best_p = normalize_nonneg(best_p)
    seq = [float(x) for x in best_p]

    try:
        exact = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        if math.isfinite(exact) and exact < best_score:
            # evaluate_sequence is the authoritative objective; use it for logging.
            # The returned sequence is unchanged.
            best_score = exact
            print(f"[final] evaluate_sequence={exact:.10f} internal={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
        # Extremely defensive fallback; should not normally happen.
        n = 2048
        seq = [1.0 / n] * n
        best_score = 2.0

    print(f"[final] returned length={len(seq)} best_score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)
    return seq
```
